The constants are kept separate so call\n82\tsites can branch on intent without coupling the two failure classes.\n83\t\"\"\"\n84\t\n85\t\n86\tEVAL_ID_REGEX: re.Pattern[str] = re.compile(\n87\t    r\"^[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9]*([0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?)?$\"\n88\t)\n89\t\"\"\"Compiled FR-SCH2 eval-id regex (single source of truth for the runtime guard).\n90\t\n91\tMatches the literal regex declared in design-spec §5 and mirrored by the\n92\t``evalIdString`` ``pattern`` inside ``suite.schema.json`` (T01.02). The\n93\tpattern accepts canonical eval ids (``E1``, ``E15``, ``D15``) and\n94\tparameterize-expanded ids (``E2.1``, ``E2.10``) while rejecting:\n95\t\n96\t* Path-traversal patterns: ``../home``, ``/etc``, ``..``, ``./foo``.\n97\t* Embedded separators: ``foo/bar``, ``foo\\\\bar``, ``E1/x``.\n98\t* Empty strings and leading-digit ids (``1E``, ``9``).\n99\t* Template tokens that leaked past parameterize expansion\n100\t  (``{{prefix}}``, ``${var}``).\n101\t\n102\tThe pattern is compiled at module import so the loader hot path (one\n103\t:func:`validate_eval_id` call per eval, plus one per parameterize-\n104\texpanded id) avoids re-parsing on every check.\n105\t\"\"\"\n106\t\n107\t\n108\tclass InvalidEvalId(Exception):\n109\t    \"\"\"Raised when an eval id fails the FR-SCH2 regex.\n110\t\n111\t    The guard is applied in two places by COMP-002 ``SuiteLoader`` (T01.07):\n112\t\n113\t    1. At loader entry, against every static id in the manifest. (The\n114\t       schema layer rejects malformed static ids too, but the runtime\n115\t       guard is the authoritative check the security model relies on.)\n116\t    2. After parameterize expansion, against every generated id (``E2.1``,\n117\t       ``E2.2``, ...). This closes the path-traversal attack surface\n118\t       where a parameterize substitution could otherwise inject ``..`` or\n119\t       ``/`` into an id that then gets interpolated into\n120\t       ``home_root / eval_id / home`` by ``HomeIsolation``.\n121\t\n122\t    The exception MUST raise before any filesystem write (NFR-SEC1\n123\t    invariant) and the CLI MUST map it to :data:`INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE`\n124\t    (= 2).\n125\t\n126\t    Attributes:\n127\t        eval_id: The exact value that failed the regex. Stored as the\n128\t            original input (``repr``-rendered in :meth:`__str__`) so\n129\t            reporters can show the offending payload verbatim — including\n130\t            quotes around whitespace or empty values — without losing\n131\t            forensic detail.\n132\t    \"\"\"\n133\t\n134\t    def __init__(self, eval_id: Any) -> None:\n135\t        self.eval_id = eval_id\n136\t        super().__init__(\n137\t            f\"eval id failed FR-SCH2 regex ({EVAL_ID_REGEX.pattern!r}): \"\n138\t            f\"{eval_id!r}\"\n139\t        )\n140\t\n141\t\n142\tdef validate_eval_id(eval_id: str) -> None:\n143\t    \"\"\"Enforce the FR-SCH2 eval-id regex.\n144\t\n145\t    The function is a pure guard: it returns ``None`` on success and\n146\t    raises :class:`InvalidEvalId` otherwise. No filesystem access, no\n147\t    logging, no globals touched — so callers can apply it freely on\n148\t    the hot path without worrying about side effects.\n149\t\n150\t    Args:\n151\t        eval_id: The eval identifier to check. On any violation raise :class:`SchemaError` with a stable field-path\n   message; the CLI catches it and exits with :data:`SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE`\n   (= 2 per the harness exit-code table in design-spec §4).\n4. The constant is exported\nso CLI callers and tests can assert against a single source of truth\nwithout duplicating the literal.\n\"\"\"\n\n\nINVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE: int = 2\n\"\"\"Process exit code emitted when :class:`InvalidEvalId` reaches the CLI boundary.\n\nFR-SCH2 / NFR-SEC1 maps every malformed eval id to the same exit code as\n:data:`SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE` (= 2) because both are surfaces of the same\n\"harness rejected the manifest before any filesystem write\" outcome from\nthe operator's point of view. The constants are kept separate so call\nsites can branch on intent without coupling the two failure classes.\n\"\"\"\n\n\nEVAL_ID_REGEX: re.Pattern[str] = re.compile(\n    r\"^[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9]*([0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?)?$\"\n)\n\"\"\"Compiled FR-SCH2 eval-id regex (single source of truth for the runtime guard).\n\nMatches the literal regex declared in design-spec §5 and mirrored by the\n``evalIdString`` ``pattern`` inside ``suite.schema.json`` (T01.02). The\npattern accepts canonical eval ids (``E1``, ``E15``, ``D15``) and\nparameterize-expanded ids (``E2.1``, ``E2.10``) while rejecting:\n\n* Path-traversal patterns: ``../home``, ``/etc``, ``..``, ``./foo``.\n* Embedded separators: ``foo/bar``, ``foo\\\\bar``, ``E1/x``.\n* Empty strings and leading-digit ids (``1E``, ``9``).\n* Template tokens that leaked past parameterize expansion\n  (``{{prefix}}``, ``${var}``).\n\nThe pattern is compiled at module import so the loader hot path (one\n:func:`validate_eval_id` call per eval, plus one per parameterize-\nexpanded id) avoids re-parsing on every check.\n\"\"\"\n\n\nclass InvalidEvalId(Exception):\n    \"\"\"Raised when an eval id fails the FR-SCH2 regex.\n\n    The guard is applied in two places by COMP-002 ``SuiteLoader`` (T01.07):\n\n    1. At loader entry, against every static id in the manifest. (The\n       schema layer rejects malformed static ids too, but the runtime\n       guard is the authoritative check the security model relies on.)\n    2. After parameterize expansion, against every generated id (``E2.1``,\n       ``E2.2``, ...). This closes the path-traversal attack surface\n       where a parameterize substitution could otherwise inject ``..`` or\n       ``/`` into an id that then gets interpolated into\n       ``home_root / eval_id / home`` by ``HomeIsolation``.\n\n    The exception MUST raise before any filesystem write (NFR-SEC1\n    invariant) and the CLI MUST map it to :data:`INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE`\n    (= 2).\n\n    Attributes:\n        eval_id: The exact value that failed the regex. Stored as the\n            original input (``repr``-rendered in :meth:`__str__`) so\n            reporters can show the offending payload verbatim — including\n            quotes around whitespace or empty values — without losing\n            forensic detail.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, eval_id: Any) -> None:\n        self.eval_id = eval_id\n        super().__init__(\n            f\"eval id failed FR-SCH2 regex ({EVAL_ID_REGEX.pattern!r}): \"\n            f\"{eval_id!r}\"\n        )\n\n\ndef validate_eval_id(eval_id: str) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Enforce the FR-SCH2 eval-id regex.\n\n    The function is a pure guard: it returns ``None`` on success and\n    raises :class:`InvalidEvalId` otherwise. No filesystem access, no\n    logging, no globals touched — so callers can apply it freely on\n    the hot path without worrying about side effects.\n\n    Args:\n        eval_id: The eval identifier to check. Schema-valid manifests\n            already constrain this to a string at the schema layer, but\n            the runtime guard does its own type check so callers that\n            bypass the schema (e.g., post-parameterize expansion code\n            paths) cannot accidentally smuggle non-string scalars\n            through.\n\n    Raises:\n        InvalidEvalId: Raised when ``eval_id`` is not a ``str`` or when\n            it does not match :data:`EVAL_ID_REGEX`. The exception\n            carries the offending value on :attr:`InvalidEvalId.eval_id`\n            and MUST be mapped by the CLI boundary to\n            :data:`INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    # Type guard first so YAML scalars that decode to int/bool/None cannot\n    # bypass the regex check via duck typing.\n    if not isinstance(eval_id, str):\n        raise InvalidEvalId(eval_id)\n\n    if EVAL_ID_REGEX.fullmatch(eval_id) is None:\n        raise InvalidEvalId(eval_id)\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass _Violation:\n    \"\"\"One jsonschema error projected into a stable, log-friendly shape.\"\"\"\n\n    path: str\n    message: str\n\n    def render(self) -> str:\n        return f\"{self.path}: {self.message}\"\n\n\nclass SchemaError(Exception):\n    \"\"\"Raised when a manifest fails ``suite.schema.json`` validation.\n\n    The exception carries the manifest path, every collected\n    :class:`jsonschema.exceptions.ValidationError` projected into a\n    field-path / message pair, and a pre-rendered multi-line ``message``\n    suitable for stderr. The CLI maps this to exit code\n    :data:`SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n\n    Attributes:\n        manifest_path: Filesystem path of the manifest that failed.\n        violations: Tuple of ``(json_path, error_message)`` pairs, one per\n            jsonschema error. Order is deterministic (sorted by JSON path)\n            so reporter output is stable across runs.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        manifest_path: Path,\n        violations: Iterable[_Violation],\n    ) -> None:\n        self.manifest_path = manifest_path\n        self.violations: tuple[tuple[str, str], ...] = tuple(\n            (v.path, v.message) for v in violations\n        )\n        lines = [f\"Manifest schema validation failed: {manifest_path}\"]\n        lines.extend(f\"  - {path}: {msg}\" for path, msg in self.violations)\n        super().__init__(\"\\n\".join(lines))\n\n\ndef _format_json_path(error: ValidationError) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Render a jsonschema error path as ``$.evals[0].id`` notation.\n\n    ``$`` denotes the manifest root so the top-level key for a missing\n    required field is also reportable (jsonschema leaves ``absolute_path``\n    empty in that case and stores the field name in ``message``).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if not error.absolute_path:\n        return \"$\"\n    parts: list[str] = [\"$\"]\n    for segment in error.absolute_path:\n        if isinstance(segment, int):\n            parts.append(f\"[{segment}]\")\n        else:\n            parts.append(f\".{segment}\")\n    return \"\".join(parts)\n\n\ndef _load_schema() -> Mapping[str, Any]:\n    return yaml.safe_load(SCHEMA_PATH.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n\n\ndef _read_manifest(path: Path) -> Mapping[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Decode the YAML manifest at ``path``.\n\n    The read is a pure ``open(...).read()`` — no scratch directories or\n    artifact paths are touched. YAML decode errors surface as\n    :class:`SchemaError` so CLI callers only have to handle a single\n    typed error to honour FR-SCH1.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    try:\n        raw = path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    except FileNotFoundError as exc:\n        violation = _Violation(path=\"$\", message=f\"manifest file not found: {exc}\")\n        raise SchemaError(path, (violation,)) from exc\n    except OSError as exc:\n        violation = _Violation(path=\"$\", message=f\"manifest read failed: {exc}\")\n        raise SchemaError(path, (violation,)) from exc\n\n    try:\n        decoded = yaml.safe_load(raw)\n    except yaml.YAMLError as exc:\n        violation = _Violation(path=\"$\", message=f\"YAML decode failed: {exc}\")\n        raise SchemaError(path, (violation,)) from exc\n\n    if not isinstance(decoded, Mapping):\n        violation = _Violation(\n            path=\"$\",\n            message=(\n                \"manifest must decode to a mapping at the top level; \"\n                f\"got {type(decoded).__name__}\"\n            ),\n        )\n        raise SchemaError(path, (violation,))\n\n    return decoded\n\n\ndef _validate_manifest_dict(path: Path) -> Mapping[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Read ``path``, decode YAML, and validate against ``suite.schema.json``.\n\n    Returns the raw manifest mapping (suite envelope + ``evals[]``) so\n    callers that need suite-level metadata (``name``, ``version``,\n    ``required_binaries``, etc.) — notably :class:`SuiteLoader` — do not\n    have to re-read or re-parse the file. This is the single ingress\n    point for FR-SCH1: every schema rejection raised inside the loader\n    flows through here.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    manifest = _read_manifest(path)\n\n    schema = _load_schema()\n    validator = Draft202012Validator(schema)\n    errors = sorted(\n        validator.iter_errors(manifest),\n        key=lambda e: (list(e.absolute_path), e.message),\n    )\n    if errors:\n        violations = [\n            _Violation(path=_format_json_path(err), message=err.message)\n            for err in errors\n        ]\n        raise SchemaError(path, violations)\n\n    return manifest\n\n\ndef validate_manifest(path: Path | str) -> list[EvalSpec]:\n    \"\"\"Validate ``path`` against ``suite.schema.json`` and return parsed evals.\n\n    Args:\n        path: Filesystem path to a ``suites/*.yaml`` manifest. ``str`` is\n            accepted for ergonomics; the value is normalised to ``Path``.\n\n    Returns:\n        A list of :class:`EvalSpec` instances, one per ``evals[]`` entry\n        in the manifest. All\nthree map to ``2`` per the design-spec §4 exit-code table so operators see\na single \"harness rejected the manifest before any filesystem write\"\noutcome regardless of which gate fired.\n\"\"\"\n\n\nSUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE: int = 2\n\"\"\"Single canonical exit code for every :class:`SuiteLoaderError` subclass.\n\n``SuiteLoader`` only ever exits with ``2`` because every loader-layer\nfailure is, from the operator's point of view, \"the harness refused to\nstart running the suite\". Every field is a verbatim\n    projection of the manifest envelope except ``evals``, which holds the\n    *expanded* eval list (parameterize rows materialised into one\n    ``EvalSpec`` per row, with ``.{index}``-suffixed ids).\n\n    Sequence fields are tuples so the dataclass is hashable and downstream\n    consumers cannot mutate them through the container.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    name: str\n    version: str\n    description: str\n    defaults: Mapping[str, Any]\n    required_binaries: tuple[Mapping[str, Any], ...]\n    optional_capabilities: tuple[Mapping[str, Any], ...]\n    evals: tuple[EvalSpec, ...]\n    source_path: Path\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass SuiteLoader:\n    \"\"\"COMP-002 orchestrator: turn a manifest path into a :class:`ParsedSuite`.\n\n    The gate ordering is fixed and documented in\n    ``artifacts/D-0006/spec.md`` (T01.07):\n\n    1. **Read + schema** (``_validate_manifest_dict``) — FR-SCH1.\n    2. **Static id regex** (``validate_eval_id`` on every ``evals[].id``) —\n       FR-SCH2 / NFR-SEC1. Re-uses validate_manifest's\n        # ingress so any FR-SCH1 rejection flows through one code path.\n        manifest = _validate_manifest_dict(manifest_path)\n        raw_evals = manifest[\"evals\"]\n\n        # Stage 3: FR-SCH2 static id guard. Apply to every manifest id\n        # BEFORE we touch the capability resolver — see the spec.md\n        # \"ordering rationale\" section for why this matters\n        # (CapabilityGates can shell out via shutil.which / MCP probes).\n        for entry in raw_evals:\n            validate_eval_id(entry.get(\"id\"))\n\n        # Stage 4: capability resolution. Each eval's ``requires`` list\n        # is funnelled through the injected resolver; the first eval\n        # with unresolved capabilities short-circuits the load.\n        for entry in raw_evals:\n            self._check_capabilities(entry)\n\n        # Stage 5: parameterize expansion + post-expansion id re-check.\n        # Static ids without ``parameterize`` are passed through; The current 1-based index\n        convention is safe by construction (digits-only suffix), but\n        the guard is mandatory because future expansion strategies\n        (e.g., named indices) could leak unsafe characters.\n\n        Substitution of ``{{key}}`` tokens inside other fields is the\n        runtime layer's responsibility, not the loader's: the schema\n        already constrained per-row keys to ``^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*$``,\n        so any token a row carries is safe to interpolate later. Default output is YAML (block style, unicode-friendly). |\n15\t| `--suites-dir` | path | no | Override the directory used for name / stem lookups. Stderr names `SuiteNotFound` and the searched directory. |\n24\t| `2` (`EVAL_NOT_FOUND_EXIT_CODE`) | `--eval` id did not match any post-expansion id. Stderr names `EvalNotFound`, the suite name, and the known id list. |\n25\t\n26\tAll three \"rejection\" codes are intentionally `2` so operators see a\n27\tsingle \"harness rejected the describe request\" outcome regardless of\n28\twhich gate fired. Lets\n37\t   operators describe an out-of-tree manifest by path without copying\n38\t   it into `suites_dir`.\n39\t2. `suites_dir / f\"{suite}.yaml\"` exists → return it. Matches the\n40\t   filename-stem convention used by built-in suites that will land in\n41\t   later milestones.\n42\t3. Scan `discover_suite_manifests(suites_dir)` and return the first\n43\t   manifest whose schema-validated `name:` field equals `suite`.\n44\t   Broken neighbours are skipped silently — only the matching manifest\n45\t   needs to validate.\n46\t\n47\tIf no rule matches, `SuiteNotFound` is raised and the CLI exits 2 with\n48\tthe typed class name on stderr.\n49\t\n50\t## Output schema\n51\t\n52\t### Default (YAML)\n53\t\n54\t`render_describe_yaml(payload)` renders block-style YAML with\n55\t`sort_keys=False` so the field ordering matches `suite.schema.json`\n56\t(envelope: `name`, `version`, `description`, `defaults`,\n57\t`required_binaries`, `optional_capabilities`, `evals`). `allow_unicode=True`\n58\tpreserves em-dashes and other non-ASCII characters that appear in real\n59\tmanifest titles.\n60\t\n61\t### `--json`\n62\t\n63\t`render_describe_json(payload)` emits `json.dumps(payload, indent=2,\n64\tsort_keys=True)` so the JSON output is byte-stable across hosts and\n65\tPython versions (verified by `test_render_describe_json_is_deterministic`\n66\tand `test_cli_describe_yaml_is_deterministic`).\n67\t\n68\t### Envelope shape (no `--eval`)\n69\t\n70\t`_parsed_suite_to_dict(parsed)` projects a `ParsedSuite` into:\n71\t\n72\t```yaml\n73\tname: <string>\n74\tversion: <string>\n75\tdescription: <string>\n76\tdefaults: <mapping>\n77\trequired_binaries: [ <requiredBinary>, ... ]\n78\toptional_capabilities: [ <optionalCapability>, ... ]\n79\tevals: [ <evalEntry>, ... ]   # post-parameterize-expansion\n80\t```\n81\t\n82\t`evals` is the **post-parameterize-expansion** list (`parsed.evals`),\n83\tnot the raw `evals[]` length. A manifest with one static row plus one\n84\tparameterized row of three values prints four `evals[]` entries with\n85\tids `E1, E2.1, E2.2, E2.3`.\n86\t\n87\t### Single-eval projection (`--eval <id>`)\n88\t\n89\t`_evalspec_to_dict(spec)` projects an `EvalSpec` into the\n90\t`evalEntry` shape (no suite envelope):\n91\t\n92\t```yaml\n93\tid: <string>             # already FR-SCH2-validated post-expansion\n94\ttitle: <string>\n95\tcategory: <string>       # omitted when \"\"\n96\trequires: [<string>, ...]   # omitted when empty\n97\ttimeout_sec: <int>       # omitted when None\n98\tisolation: <mapping>     # omitted when None\n99\tinputs: [<mapping>, ...] # omitted when empty\n100\texpects: [<mapping>, ...] # omitted when empty\n101\tparameterize: [<mapping>, ...]  # preserved on expanded entries\n102\t```\n103\t\n104\tOptional fields are omitted when they hold the dataclass default\n105\t(`category=\"\"`, empty sequences, `timeout_sec=None`). This honours the\n106\tT01.22 Notes contract: \"Output mirrors validated manifest exactly; no\n107\teditorial transformation\" — an eval that did not declare `category:`\n108\tin YAML does not gain one in the description.\n109\t\n110\t## Validation order (FR-CLI3 AC)\n111\t\n112\t`describe_suite()` runs the full `SuiteLoader.load()` gate chain\n113\t**before any rendering**:\n114\t\n115\t1. Resolve `--suite` to a manifest path (no FS write).\n116\t2. `SuiteLoader.load()` — schema (FR-SCH1) → static-id regex\n117\t   (FR-SCH2) → capability resolution (COMP-009 surface) →\n118\t   parameterize expansion → expanded-id regex re-check (FR-SCH2).\n119\t3. Optional `--eval` id lookup against the post-expansion list.\n120\t4. Render YAML / JSON.\n121\t\n122\tValidation rejections raise from step 2 — `click.echo` of the payload\n123\tin step 4 is never reached. Verified by\n124\t`test_cli_describe_validation_runs_before_any_stdout`, which asserts\n125\t`result.stdout == \"\"` on a schema-rejection path.\n126\t\n127\t## Loader integration\n128\t\n129\t`describe_suite(suite, *, suites_dir, eval_id=None, loader=None)` is\n130\tthe function the CLI handler delegates to. The `loader` parameter is\n131\tinjectable so tests can stub `SuiteLoader` without touching the file\n132\tsystem. The default loader uses `PermissiveCapabilityResolver` (same\n133\tas `eval list`) — capability gating for `describe` is intentionally\n134\tpermissive because describing a manifest is a read-only operation that\n135\tshould not require the runtime binary set on the host.\n136\t\n137\t## Acceptance criteria → implementation map\n138\t\n139\t| AC bullet (T01.22) | Implementation site |\n140\t|---|---|\n141\t| `superclaude eval describe --suite <name>` prints validated post-parameterize manifest content. | `eval_describe` Click handler + `describe_suite` + `render_describe_yaml`; Verified by snapshotting the default scratch root\n   and a per-test sandbox.\n\nCross-link map (every test cites the AC by ID in its docstring so the\ntraceability matrix in ``artifacts/D-0020/spec.md`` is grep-able):\n\n* FR-SCH1 → ``validate_manifest`` schema rejections\n* FR-SCH2 → ``validate_eval_id`` regex + SuiteLoader post-expansion re-check\n* NFR-SEC1 → no-FS-write invariant on every rejection path\n* TEST-001 → this module (owning AC)\n\nThis module deliberately overlaps with the function-surface unit tests\nin ``test_schema_validate.py``, ``test_eval_id_regex.py``, and\n``test_path_traversal.py`` so that a single CI failure here is enough\nto surface a regression in any of the three gates — operators reading\nthe failure log get the FR / NFR ID directly from the test docstring\ninstead of having to cross-reference three separate suites.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport shutil\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Iterator\nfrom unittest.mock import patch\n\nimport pytest\nfrom click.testing import CliRunner\n\nfrom superclaude.cli.eval import (\n    INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE,\n    SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE,\n    SUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE,\n    EvalSpec,\n    InvalidEvalId,\n    SchemaError,\n    SuiteLoader,\n    eval_group,\n    validate_eval_id,\n    validate_manifest,\n)\n\nFIXTURES_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent / \"fixtures\"\nDEFAULT_SCRATCH_ROOT = Path(\"/tmp/eval-runs\")\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Helpers\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef _scratch_snapshot() -> set[str]:\n    \"\"\"Snapshot the default scratch root for write-leak detection.\n\n    ``/tmp/eval-runs`` is the FR-SCH1 / NFR-SEC1 default scratch root;\n    no rejection path may touch it. 2026-05-20\n**Tier:** STRICT (security)\n\n## Purpose\n\nThe TEST-001 module is the **CLI-boundary rejection matrix** that\nbinds the three foundational security gates of the cliEval loader\npipeline raises.\n\nIt overlaps deliberately with the function-surface unit tests in\n`test_schema_validate.py` (T01.04), `test_eval_id_regex.py` (T01.05),\n`test_suite_loader.py` (T01.07), and `test_path_traversal.py` (T01.08).\nA single CI failure here surfaces a regression in any of those\nupstream gates with the FR / NFR ID printed in the test docstring, so\noperators reading the failure inside `SuiteLoader._expand_entry` |\n| **NFR-SEC1** | T01.08 (D-0007) | \"No FS write before rejection\" invariant verified by snapshotting per-test sandbox + `/tmp/eval-runs` |\n| **COMP-002** | The TEST-001 matrix reuses the negative\nfixtures introduced for T01.04 (D-0004) and T01.07 (D-0006) so the\nschema-layer evidence is shared across the suite.\n\n## Exit-code contract\n\n| Class | Constant | Value | Surfaces |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `SchemaError` | `SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE` | `2` | `validate_manifest`, `SuiteLoader.load`, CLI `eval describe`, CLI `eval list`. |\n| `InvalidEvalId` | `INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE` | `2` | `validate_eval_id`, `SuiteLoader.load` (pre- and post-expansion), CLI `eval describe`. |\n| Any loader-layer rejection | `SUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE` | `2` | Aggregate alias used by CLI handlers in `commands.py`. |\n\nAll three collapse to `2` per design-spec §4 exit-code table. TEST-001\nparametrizes the assertion over all three constants to pin the\ncontract against drift.\n\n## Pre-flight ordering invariant\n\nNFR-SEC1 demands that every rejection path raises before any\nfilesystem write. TEST-001 verifies this with two complementary\nmechanisms:\n\n1. **Per-test sandbox snapshot** via the `sandbox_snapshot` fixture —\n   captures `tmp_path.rglob(\"*\")` before and after the rejection-\n   under-test. Empty delta = no writes.\n2. **Default scratch-root snapshot** via the module-level\n   `_scratch_snapshot()` helper — captures `/tmp/eval-runs` (the\n   FR-SCH1 default scratch root) before and after. Empty delta = no\n   writes outside the per-test sandbox.\n\nThe CLI-level `test_no_fs_write_when_cli_describe_rejects` test adds a\nthird assertion: `result.stdout == \"\"` confirms the rejection raised\nbefore `click.echo` ever wrote the rendered payload to stdout. This\ncloses the operator-visible side of the invariant — a regression that\nlet a rejection leak even a single byte of stdout would trip this\nassertion before it could trip the scratch-root snapshot.\n\n## Out of scope for TEST-001\n\n- **Capability-resolution rejection (`UnresolvedCapability`)** — owned\n  by `test_suite_loader.py` (T01.07). TEST-001's mandate per the\n  T01.23 deliverable text is schema errors, unsafe IDs, parameterized\n  IDs, and pre-flight ordering. The unresolved-capability route shares\n  the `SUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE` constant, so the\n  `test_every_rejection_exit_code_is_two` invariant transitively\n  covers its exit-code claim.\n- **`eval run` rejection** — the runner CLI (M2) reuses\n  `SuiteLoader.load`, so the same exit-2 mapping applies; the\n  CLI-level binding lands when the `eval run` command does.\n- **Scratch-root allowlist enforcement** — owned by\n  `test_scratch_root_allowlist.py` (T01.19). TEST-001 only asserts\n  \"no writes to `/tmp/eval-runs`\"; the allowlist test owns the\n  positive contract.\n\n## Sub-agent verification\n\nT01.23 is tier-STRICT with sub-agent delegation marked \"Recommended\".\nPer Section 5.6 of the task-builder protocol, the author satisfies\nsub-agent verification by:\n\n1. Pins `additionalProperties: false` at the envelope. |\n| `test_schema_violation_cli_describe_exits_two` | `superclaude eval describe` | Exit code `2`, `SchemaError` named on stderr. |\n| `test_schema_violation_cli_list_exits_two` | `superclaude eval list` | Exit code `2`, `SchemaError` named on stderr. Confirms `list` shares the same exit-mapping as `describe`. |\n\n### AC bullet 2 — unsafe-id rejection (FR-SCH2)\n\n| Test | Surface | Asserts |\n|---|---|---|\n| `test_unsafe_id_rejected_by_validate_eval_id[…]` (parametrized × 6) | `validate_eval_id` | Each NFR-SEC1 named case (`../home`, `/etc`, `..`, empty, `1bad`, `{{prefix}}`) raises `InvalidEvalId` with the offending payload preserved. |\n| `test_unsafe_id_rejected_by_suite_loader_before_capability_resolution` | `SuiteLoader.load` | A mocked `_RecordingResolver` records zero calls after the rejection — pins the COMP-002 gate-ordering contract (id regex < capability resolve). |\n| `test_unsafe_id_cli_describe_exits_two` | `superclaude eval describe` | Lowercase id (`lowercase-bad`) → exit `2`, stderr contains `InvalidEvalId` OR `SchemaError` (either route is acceptable; both block at exit `2`). |\n\n### AC bullet 3 — parameterize expansion validated post-expansion\n\n| Test | Surface | Asserts |\n|---|---|---|\n| `test_parameterize_safe_expansion_produces_dot_index_ids` | `SuiteLoader.load` | Reference fixture's 3-row `parameterize` on `E2` emits `[\"E1\", \"E2.1\", \"E2.2\", \"E2.3\"]`. Each expanded id is re-validated by `validate_eval_id` to confirm the loader's post-expansion re-check accepts safe ids. |\n| `test_parameterize_unsafe_expansion_is_rejected_post_expansion` | `SuiteLoader.load` (mock-injected hostile `_expand_entry`) | Defence-in-depth: a simulated hostile expansion producing `E2.../../etc/passwd` MUST raise `InvalidEvalId` before any downstream consumer runs. |\n\n### AC bullet 4 — pre-flight ordering / no FS writes before rejection (NFR-SEC1)\n\n| Test | Surface | Asserts |\n|---|---|---|\n| `test_no_fs_write_when_schema_rejected` | `validate_manifest` | Per-test sandbox + `/tmp/eval-runs` snapshot delta is empty after a `SchemaError`. |\n| `test_no_fs_write_when_unsafe_id_rejected` | `validate_eval_id` | Per-test sandbox + `/tmp/eval-runs` snapshot delta is empty after an `InvalidEvalId`. |\n| `test_no_fs_write_when_cli_describe_rejects` | `superclaude eval describe` | Exit `2`, `result.stdout == \"\"`, `/tmp/eval-runs` unchanged. End-to-end CLI binding of the invariant. |\n\n### Cross-cutting\n\n| Test | Asserts |\n|---|---|\n| `test_every_rejection_exit_code_is_two` (parametrized × 3) | All three loader-layer exit-code constants (`SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE`, `INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE`, `SUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE`) equal `2`. |\n\n## Fixtures consumed\n\n| Fixture | Role |\n|---|---|\n| `tests/cli/eval/fixtures/missing_name_suite.yaml` | FR-SCH1 missing-required-field rejection (function + CLI surfaces). |\n| `tests/cli/eval/fixtures/unknown_top_level_suite.yaml` | FR-SCH1 `additionalProperties: false` rejection. |\n| `tests/cli/eval/fixtures/invalid_eval_entry_suite.yaml` | FR-SCH2 unsafe-id rejection at the CLI surface (lowercase id trips schema and/or runtime regex). |\n| `tests/cli/eval/fixtures/valid_suite.yaml` | FR-SCH2 safe post-expansion ids (`E2.1, E2.2, E2.3`). |\n\nNo new fixtures are required. The TEST-001 matrix reuses the negative\nfixtures introduced for T01.04 (D-0004) and T01.07 (D-0006) so the\nschema-layer evidence is shared across the suite.\n\n## Exit-code contract\n\n| Class | Constant | Value | Surfaces |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `SchemaError` | `SCHEMA_ERROR_EXIT_CODE` | `2` | `validate_manifest`, `SuiteLoader.load`, CLI `eval describe`, CLI `eval list`. |\n| `InvalidEvalId` | `INVALID_EVAL_ID_EXIT_CODE` | `2` | `validate_eval_id`, `SuiteLoader.load` (pre- and post-expansion), CLI `eval describe`. |\n| Any loader-layer rejection | `SUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE` | `2` | Aggregate alias used by CLI handlers in `commands.py`. |\n\nAll three collapse to `2` per design-spec §4 exit-code table. TEST-001\nparametrizes the assertion over all three constants to pin the\ncontract against drift.\n\n## Pre-flight ordering invariant\n\nNFR-SEC1 demands that every rejection path raises before any\nfilesystem write. TEST-001 verifies this with two complementary\nmechanisms:\n\n1. **Per-test sandbox snapshot** via the `sandbox_snapshot` fixture —\n   captures `tmp_path.rglob(\"*\")` before and after the rejection-\n   under-test. Empty delta = no writes.\n2. **Default scratch-root snapshot** via the module-level\n   `_scratch_snapshot()` helper — captures `/tmp/eval-runs` (the\n   FR-SCH1 default scratch root) before and after. Empty delta = no\n   writes outside the per-test sandbox.\n\nThe CLI-level `test_no_fs_write_when_cli_describe_rejects` test adds a\nthird assertion: `result.stdout == \"\"` confirms the rejection raised\nbefore `click.echo` ever wrote the rendered payload to stdout. This\ncloses the operator-visible side of the invariant — a regression that\nlet a rejection leak even a single byte of stdout would trip this\nassertion before it could trip the scratch-root snapshot.\n\n## Out of scope for TEST-001\n\n- **Capability-resolution rejection (`UnresolvedCapability`)** — owned\n  by `test_suite_loader.py` (T01.07). TEST-001's mandate per the\n  T01.23 deliverable text is schema errors, unsafe IDs, parameterized\n  IDs, and pre-flight ordering. The unresolved-capability route shares\n  the `SUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE` constant, so the\n  `test_every_rejection_exit_code_is_two` invariant transitively\n  covers its exit-code claim.\n- **`eval run` rejection** — the runner CLI (M2) reuses\n  `SuiteLoader.load`, so the same exit-2 mapping applies; the\n  CLI-level binding lands when the `eval run` command does.\n- **Scratch-root allowlist enforcement** — owned by\n  `test_scratch_root_allowlist.py` (T01.19). TEST-001 only asserts\n  \"no writes to `/tmp/eval-runs`\"; the allowlist test owns the\n  positive contract.\n\n## Sub-agent verification\n\nT01.23 is tier-STRICT with sub-agent delegation marked \"Recommended\".\nPer Section 5.6 of the task-builder protocol, the author satisfies\nsub-agent verification by:\n\n1. Their\nfunction-surface tests verify the slice in isolation. TEST-001 is the\n**integration matrix** that ties those slices together at the CLI\nboundary so the operator-visible contract — \"harness exits 2 with a\ntyped error class on stderr and no FS writes before rejection\" — is\nverified end-to-end.\n\nThe matrix-style approach means a single CI failure in\n`test_schema_id_rejection.py` surfaces a regression in any of the four\nupstream tasks. The docstring on every test names the FR / NFR ID, so\nthe failing test log identifies the failing gate without requiring a\ncross-suite lookup.\n\n## CLI-surface vs function-surface coverage\n\nTEST-001 invokes the CLI through `click.testing.CliRunner` rather than\nspawning a real subprocess. The CliRunner-based approach is consistent\nwith the patterns established by `test_describe.py` and `test_list.py`\n(T01.21 / T01.22) and avoids the cost of spinning up a subprocess\nper-test on a hot path. The exit-code contract is verified by\n`result.exit_code` against the canonical exit-code constants exported\nfrom `superclaude.cli.eval`.\n\n## Mocking strategy for the post-expansion re-check\n\nThe \"unsafe parameterize expansion\" test mocks `_expand_entry` so the\ntest can simulate a hostile (or buggy) expansion strategy without\nintroducing a malicious fixture. The current `.{index}` expansion\nconvention is safe by construction; the test exists to pin the\nload-bearing invariant that the loader applies `validate_eval_id`\nAFTER expansion, not just before. A future change to the expansion\nstrategy (e.g., named indices, parameter-driven suffixes) cannot\nsilently bypass the re-check without tripping this test.\n\n## Snapshot baseline strategy\n\nThe NFR-SEC1 invariant tests use two snapshot mechanisms:\n\n1. Per-test `tmp_path` snapshot — captured via the `sandbox_snapshot`\n   fixture that yields `(sandbox_path, baseline_set)`. Tests compare\n   `set(rglob(\"*\"))` before and after; an empty delta confirms no\n   writes leaked into the per-test sandbox.\n2. Default scratch root (`/tmp/eval-runs`) snapshot — captured via the\n   module-level `_scratch_snapshot()` helper. The directory is not\n   guaranteed to exist on a clean dev box, so an empty set is treated\n   as a valid baseline. Only the *delta* matters.\n\nThe two snapshots complement each other: the per-test sandbox catches\nleaks into the test's own working directory (e.g., a stray\n`Path.write_text(...)` against a relative path), and the scratch-root\nsnapshot catches leaks into the production default location.\n\n## Reuse vs duplication\n\n`test_path_traversal.py` (T01.08) already contains the\nper-named-case NFR-SEC1 checklist. TEST-001 deliberately re-runs a\nsubset of those cases through a single parametrized test so the\nTEST-001 file has a self-contained assertion for each AC bullet —\nT01.23's AC text reads \"tests for schema-violation rejection, unsafe\nid rejection, parameterize expansion validated post-expansion, and\npre-flight ordering\", which implies an in-file checklist. The\nduplication is bounded (one parametrized case in TEST-001 vs seven\nindividual tests in T01.08) and the trade is intentional.\n\n## CLI rejection surfaces covered\n\n| Command | Test |\n|---|---|\n| `eval describe --suite <broken>` | `test_schema_violation_cli_describe_exits_two`, `test_unsafe_id_cli_describe_exits_two`, `test_no_fs_write_when_cli_describe_rejects` |\n| `eval list --suites-dir <broken>` | `test_schema_violation_cli_list_exits_two` |\n\n`eval doctor` is not covered here because doctor does not load suite\nmanifests; its rejection surface is the capability gate (covered by\n`test_doctor.py`, T01.13).\n\n## Open follow-ups\n\nNone. The 20-case suite passes green on first run and the full\n`tests/cli/eval/` suite expanded from 299 → 319 passing with zero\nregressions.
